# Sweep #6 · Terminal 21 — the database, migrations 001 to 118

**Territory:** the first 120 files of `supabase/migrations/` (`001_menu_items.sql` … `118_price_order_tenant_scope.sql`).
**Branch:** `sweep6/t21-db-migrations-a` · **worktree:** `sweep6/T21` · **port:** 4121 (never 4000).
**Phase IDs P10001–P10500 are permanent. Never renumber them.** The next sweep re-runs these rows FIRST, then adds new ones.

## How the 500 are budgeted

| Group | IDs | Count | Share | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | P10001–P10120 | 120 | 24% | Reading the code for correctness — one row per file: every object it declares is accounted for |
| B | P10121–P10210 | 90 | 18% | Reading the code for correctness — three deep checks on each of the 30 largest files |
| C | P10211–P10300 | 90 | 18% | Conformance to the project's own written rules (RLS + grant, indexes, idempotency, re-runnability, additive schema) |
| D | P10301–P10375 | 75 | 15% | Watching it run — headless Playwright on port 4121 |
| E | P10376–P10425 | 50 | 10% | Screenshots opened and read (desktop 1280×800 and Samsung A35 360×780 dpr3) |
| F | P10426–P10475 | 50 | 10% | Tracing one change across panels |
| G | P10476–P10500 | 25 | 5% | My own judgment about whether a real restaurant needs it to work this way |

Correctness reading is A+B = 210 rows (42%), conformance is C = 90 rows (18%). Every file in the territory has at least one row; the 30 largest have ten or more between A, B and C.

## Result of this run: 500 rows · 498 green · 0 red · 2 skipped

Skipped rows and why:

- **P10479** — outside my territory (public/panels/editor/app.js); raised as a handoff, not changed
- **P10481** — app/api/admin/floor/route.ts:74 calls lfh_floor_state() with no restaurant; unreachable today (the only caller uses ?all=1). Handoff.

Migration 358 was added after the owner reviewed this run and approved the restaurant_id default fix (P10480).

Ten problems were found and fixed during this run (migrations 003, 005, 008, 015, 036, 037, 040, 078, 080, 083); the rows that
caught them are C-group rows from P10211 tagged "Running … ALONE". They are green here because the fix is in this branch.

The wordmark and hero render as stated, but the tagline on screen is BONJOUR, not the BONSOIR in restaurants.tagline. Deliberate: components/MenuView.tsx renders `isDefault ? t.greeting : (tagline ·· "Welcome")`, so restaurant #1 keeps its localised hero from lib/i18n.ts and only OTHER restaurants read the column. That line carries a REJECTED (owner, 2026-08-17) comment and docs/REJECTED-IDEAS.md R30 confirms it. Proved the other half on Pizza Palace, whose own BUONASERA DOES reach the screen. |

# SWEEP #7 · Terminal 21 — the same territory, re-run and extended

**Re-run 2026-08-28 against `origin/main` c005b3d3, on the dev database, from worktree `../wt-s7-t21`, port 4221.**

## What the re-run of P10001–P10500 found

| | |
|---|---|
| Rows re-run | **500 of 500** |
| **REGRESSIONS (✅ last time, ❌ now)** | **0** |
| Long-standing `⏭` rows CLOSED | **2** — P10479 (migration 359 dropped the two dead columns and the editor's boxes are gone) and P10481 (the one real unscoped call site is fixed and `verify:rpc-scoped` guards all 116) |
| Expectations MOVED because the product or the instrument improved | **6 in group A** (a more complete object count) + **8 elsewhere**, each said so in its own row |
| Rows I first read as RED and then cleared | **10** — every one was my own detector, not the product. Each row records what was wrong with the check. |

**The single most useful thing this re-run found is not a product fault.** Rows P10001–P10120 — 24% of
this whole territory — all read `node scripts/verify-migration-truth.mjs --only <file>`, and **that
script existed in no commit in this repository.** It was written in sweep #6's throw-away worktree and
thrown away with it, so the largest group of checks on the database could not be re-run at all. It is
now a committed guard, `npm run verify:migration-truth`, and it covers all 375 migration files rather
than the 120 this territory owns: **340 declared objects in this range, 1,104 across the folder, none
missing.**

### What the re-run took me on

- `verify:run-alone` green (19 retired objects checked across 375 files; 191 live functions, no stale signature).
- `verify:grants` green (47-entry allow-list, 174 expected functions all present, 6 cron jobs, RLS on every table).
- `verify:rpc-scoped` green (57 scoped RPCs, 116 call sites, none relying on the default).
- `verify:migration-truth` green (new).
- Ten live surfaces on port 4221, twice each, plus four tenants' own menu doors; ten screenshots taken and seven opened and read.

## The 500 new checks — P25101–P25600

Planned after reading the files, to cover ground the first 500 did not. Group A only ever asked "does
the object still EXIST"; nothing had ever asked whether it still has the same **shape**.

| group | ids | count | what it asks |
|---|---|---|---|
| N1 | P25101–P25173 | 73 | Every function this range created: is its live BODY still the newest migration's body, statement by statement? |
| N2 | P25174–P25263 | 90 | Every column added: same type, still there? |
| N3 | P25264–P25301 | 38 | Every index: same columns, same uniqueness? |
| N4 | P25302–P25325 | 24 | Every trigger: same table, same timing, same events, same function? |
| N5 | P25326–P25334 | 9 | Every read/write rule: same command? |
| N6 | P25335–P25366 | 32 | Every GRANT to the guest key: still the sequence's last word? |
| N7 | P25367–P25374 | 8 | Every named constraint: still there, still the same kind? |
| N8 | P25375–P25414 | 40 | The DATA: orphans, restaurant mismatches, impossible states |
| N9 | P25415–P25429 | 15 | The NUMBERS: one series per restaurant per business day, no repeats |
| N10 | P25430–P25444 | 15 | Obituary truth: every "this is retired" comment is true today |
| N11 | P25445–P25564 | 120 | Every one of the 120 files, applied twice, lands in the same state |
| N12 | P25565–P25596 | 32 | Four tenants' menus, rendered, compared to their own database rows |
| N13 | P25597–P25600 | 4 | My own judgment about whether a real restaurant needs it this way |

**Result: 500 written, 500 executed, 500 ✅ — after clearing 24 rows that my own checks got wrong
before the product did.** Every corrected check says in its own row what was wrong with it, because a
detector that cries wolf costs the next sweep more than a gap does.

| P25413 | The data in the tables migrations 001-118 created holds together: an order marked paid with no time of payment (migration 112) | one scoped COUNT per question against the dev database; each must come back zero | ✅ | re-run 2026-09-15 (sweep #9 T30) — ❌ ON THE FIRST RE-RUN, FIXED THE SAME RUN, NOW ✅. 167 orders were marked paid with no paid_at — none from the product (all three settle paths write the pair together), all from four fixture scripts: seed-demo-orders, verify-customer-erase, t9-fixture-test, stress/stress-max. No screen showed a wrong number, because every owner money function resolves the date with CASE WHEN khata_at IS NOT NULL AND paid_at IS NOT NULL THEN paid_at ELSE created_at END — but lfh_khata_collected filters on paid_at IS NOT NULL outright, so a seeded bill vanished from a collection report. All five scripts now stamp paid_at, the 167 rows were backfilled to their own created_at, and verify:test-safety section 13 keeps it that way. (T30 item 2.) Six rows have paid_at 26–60ms BEFORE created_at — a client clock vs a database default, sub-100ms, not a fault. |

| P25600 | The dev database carries hundreds of other terminals' fixture rows with no owner and no expiry. Does that hurt anything a real restaurant would feel? | my own reading of the code and the data against what a real restaurant needs; each verdict names its evidence | ✅ | Not the product — a real restaurant never sees them. It hurts every SWEEP: four of this run's six red rows were this residue, and each one cost a real investigation to clear. Carried up as a decision, not a fault. |

## SWEEP #9 · TERMINAL 29 — the fifty new phases, 2026-09-15 (`P104401`–`P104500`)

**Territory:** positions 1–80 of `ls supabase/migrations/*.sql | sort` (`001_menu_items.sql` …
`078_tenancy_core.sql`). **Branch** `sweep9/t29-migrations-1-80` · **worktree** `../wt-s9-t29` ·
**port 4429** (never 4000). Ids `P104401`–`P104500` were pre-allocated to this terminal; **50 used,
`P104451`–`P104500` left free.**

**The 432 rows already covering these files were re-run FIRST** and their results updated in place
above (420 in this file by subject, 11 more whose subject is a function these files define, and
`P06557` in `T14.md`). **Eight of them read green and were not**: `P25457`, `P25458`, `P25474`,
`P25476`, `P25481`, `P25498`, `P25501` and `P10249`. Each is annotated on its own row.

**Why these fifty, and not fifty others.** Rows per file across all 44 ledgers, by subject, measured
before planning: seventeen files carry **2 rows each** (007, 016, 017, 021, 023, 025, 026, 027, 028,
033, 038, 039, 044, 050, 061, 065, 072) against **32** for `014_sessions_v2_schema.sql`, **24** for
`037_billing_feedback_backend_stubs.sql` and **20** for `071_platform_orders.sql`. Group B goes to
sixteen of the seventeen thin ones. Group A goes somewhere no row had ever stood: **a file can be
perfectly idempotent and still be unrunnable today**, because a LATER migration retired what it
creates — which is how three of these eighty files came to raise a hard error on every live database.

**Re-run:** `node scripts/verify-migrations-1-80.mjs` (A, A2, B, D, E — 42 rows) and, with the app up
on 4429, `node scripts/sweep/t29-live.mjs` (C — 8 rows). Result of this run: **50 of 50 green**,
with the seven fixes in this branch.

## SWEEP #9 · TERMINAL 29 · **ROUND 2** — the whole 500, 2026-09-16 (`P104451`–`P104500` + `P148001`–`P148450`)

**Territory:** unchanged — positions 1–80 of `ls supabase/migrations/*.sql | sort`
(`001_menu_items.sql` … `078_tenancy_core.sql`). **Branch** `sweep9/t29-r2` · **port 4429**.

**Ids: 500, none claimed from the registry.** `P104451`–`P104500` is this terminal's unused round-1
tail; `P148001`–`P148450` is its pre-allocated round-2 block from INDEX.md's table. 50 + 450 = 500.
`P148451`–`P149000` remain free.

**Result: 480 green · 20 skipped · 0 red.** Every skip is an object a later migration deliberately
retired, named on its own row. **No product fault was found in this territory in round 2.**

### Why these five hundred, and not five hundred others

Measured before planning, not chosen by having an idea (rule 2b). Rows per file across all 46
ledgers, by subject: **712 rows touch these eighty files, and they are piled up** —
`001_menu_items.sql` carries 165 and `036_kot_bill_staff_orders.sql` 37, while **twenty-six files
carry three or fewer** and `048_set_member_name.sql` carries two. Round 1 already sent sixteen
checks to the thin end.

So round 2 went after a different gap: not thin FILES but **thin QUESTIONS**. Every pre-existing row
over this territory asks one of four things — *is the object still there* (`verify:migration-truth`),
*who may run it* (`verify:grants`), *is the file re-runnable*, *does the rule still hold*. **Nothing
had ever asked, per object: is it still the SHAPE the file declared?** A column can be present and
have lost its `NOT NULL`. An index can be present and have stopped being `UNIQUE`. A trigger can be
present and fire on the wrong events. All three answer *"present"* correctly.

| group | ids | rows | what it asks |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | `P104451`–`P148102` | 152 | the SHAPE of every declared object — 24 tables, 72 columns, 26 indexes, 21 triggers, 9 policies, 1 view |
| B | `P148103`–`P148161` | 59 | every function these files define still carries its own rule, read from the LIVE body |
| C | `P148162`–`P148221` | 60 | the door on each function today, against what its own migration fitted |
| D | `P148222`–`P148308` | 87 | tenancy — per table, and per function that takes a restaurant |
| E | `P148309`–`P148388` | 80 | run-alone, **one row per file**, all eighty |
| G | `P148389`–`P148419` | 31 | the questions only a person asks — money, numbering, a table and the party on it |
| F | `P148420`–`P148450` | 31 | **driven in a real browser** on port 4429, screenshots kept |

(152 + 59 + 60 + 87 + 80 + 31 + 31 = 500. Group A spans the seam: its first fifty ids are the
round-1 tail `P104451`–`P104500`, its remaining 102 run `P148001`–`P148102`.)

**Re-run:** `node scripts/sweep/t29r2/checks.mjs` (469) and, with the app up on 4429,
`node scripts/sweep/t29r2/live.mjs` (31). Both take `--ledger` to reprint this table.

### Six checks in this round were wrong before the territory was

Worth recording, because a check that cries wolf costs more than no check. Each was corrected and
the corrected version is what ran:

- **`lfh_next_counter` "lost daily_counters"** — migration 080+ made it a one-line delegate to
  `lfh_next_counter_on`; the table is one layer down. The expectation was too literal.
- **`lfh_staff_edit_item_note` "lost order_paid"** — it no longer REFUSES a settled bill; a later
  migration made it patch only that line and flag `settled` for the audit. A design change, not a
  lost rule.
- **`lfh_kitchen_tickets` "lost order_items"** — it reads `order_dish_lines`, a later view that
  UNIONs the dish rows with the JSON ticket so a legacy order still reaches the kitchen.
- **six functions "reachable with the public menu key"** — all trigger functions and one IMMUTABLE
  arithmetic helper. A trigger function cannot be called directly at all, so no migration grants or
  revokes one. Exempted BY SHAPE from the catalogue, never by a hand-written list.
- **`restaurants` "carries no restaurant_id"** — it is the tenant ROOT; it has `id`.
- **`019`/`037` "put back a retired function"** — `lfh_set_cart` and `lfh_next_seq` each GREW an
  argument, which is `DROP` then `CREATE` of a different signature. Tracking names alone read that
  as a retirement.

And one that was right about the shape and wrong about the cause: `idx_verification_contact` read as
MISSING until this pass was taught that **dropping a table takes its indexes with it** — migration
384 dropped `verification_codes` the previous day. That is the same lesson item 8 taught
`verify:migration-truth`, needed again one day later in a second place.

### The one money question this round asked, and its answer

`lfh_reprice_order` and `lfh_delete_order_item` both contain `v_rate numeric := 0.05`. If that
reached the money, editing an order at a restaurant on 18% GST would silently re-tax the bill at 5%.
**It does not:** both overwrite it with `lfh_effective_tax_rate(...)` before a single figure is
computed, and both handle tax-exempt and tax-included dishes. The literal is a dead initialiser.
Checked before filing, and not filed.
